Current Meeting


Meeting Opening

CEO Vesna Jovanović opened the meeting with the following statement:

Welcome everyone to our annual budget meeting for Year 1. Thank you all for joining today.

Our company currently has $100000.00 in total assets. We are operating in a stable market environment this year. A balanced market environment characterized by steady economic growth, consistent interest rates, and low volatility across all quarters. In this predictable landscape, stocks offer balanced potential for both gains and losses with moderate volatility, while real estate tends to maintain steady appreciation with occasional flat periods. Bonds generally deliver consistent returns with minimal risk, though they yield less than growth-oriented assets in favorable conditions. Cash savings provide complete safety but offer the lowest returns, serving primarily for capital preservation rather than growth. The minimal seasonal variations create relatively uniform investment opportunities throughout the year.

As this is our first year, we do not have historical performance data yet. We'll need to base our decisions on theoretical risk-return profiles and current market conditions.

The today, we need to decide how we'll allocate our investment budget across the four quarters of this year. We have four options to consider:

- Option A: We allocate 50% of our total funds, evenly distributed across the four quarters (12.5% per quarter).
- Option B: We allocate 50% of our total funds with an increasing allocation: 5% in Q1, 10% in Q2, 15% in Q3, and 20% in Q4.
- Option C: We allocate 100% of our total funds, evenly distributed across the four quarters (25% per quarter).
- Option D: We allocate 100% of our total funds with an increasing allocation: 10% in Q1, 20% in Q2, 30% in Q3, and 40% in Q4.

Each option presents different levels of risk and opportunity given the current market conditions.

The objective of today's meeting is to thoroughly discuss these options and come to a decision that best serves our company's interests in this stable market environment. We'll need to balance potential returns with the associated risks. We'll have an open discussion, then I'll propose what I believe is the best approach based on our collective input, and we'll take a vote requiring a two-thirds majority to proceed.

Let's begin our discussion with your initial thoughts on these options.


CEO's Initial Position Assessment

Before discussion began, CEO Vesna Jovanović formed an initial position:

Vesna Jovanović CONFIDENCE DISTRIBUTION:
- Option A: 60%
- Option B: 25%
- Option C: 10%
- Option D: 5%
PREFERRED OPTION: Option A
REASONING: I am most comfortable with Option A because it allows us to participate in the market while keeping half of our capital safe, which feels appropriate given the lack of historical data and my preference for stability. Even distribution helps avoid surprises and fits my need for predictability—I'm not interested in taking unnecessary risks or experimenting with aggressive strategies this early. Although I like to make bold moves socially, when it comes to money, I prefer tried-and-true methods and routine. The thought of risking everything right away makes me anxious, so Option A gives me peace of mind and a sense of control, even if it means slower growth.

Round 1 Summary


CEO Vesna Jovanović summarized: "Vesna Jovanović **Round 1 Summary – Annual Budget Meeting, Year 1**

1. **Restatement of Theme and Objective:**
The focus of this round was an initial assessment of each budget allocation strategy—Option A, Option B, Option C, and Option D—in the context of a stable market and no historical data. The objective was to build a shared understanding of the risk, safety, and growth potential associated with each option.

2. **Summary of Key Viewpoints:**
Every shareholder who spoke—Juriaan Osei, Benoît Ndlovu, Hiranur Tadesse, Roksana Haghighi, and Jarynthi Abebe—expressed clear support for Option A. No participant advocated for Option B, C, or D. The discussion consistently emphasized the importance of safety, predictability, and routine, with all shareholders aligning closely with my initial perspective.

3. **Arguments and Evidence Presented:**
- **Option A:**
- Allows participation in the market while keeping half of the capital safe.
- Provides flexibility to adapt to new information or market changes.
- Offers predictability, routine, and peace of mind.
- Reduces risk of surprises, anxiety, and internal conflict.
- Builds trust, discipline, and a strong team foundation.
- Supports financial agility for emerging opportunities.
- **Options B, C, D:**
- Viewed as less favorable or inappropriate at this stage.
- C and D considered riskier and likely to increase anxiety and exposure.
- B seen as more creative but less simple or predictable.
- No substantial positive arguments were presented for these alternatives.

4. **Areas of Consensus and Disagreement:**
There was unanimous agreement in favor of Option A. All participants prioritized safety, stability, and predictability, given the absence of historical data. No disagreements or advocacy for other options emerged during this round.

5. **Assessment of Progress:**
We have made significant progress in establishing a shared understanding of the risk and safety profiles of each option. The group has reached a strong consensus around Option A as the most prudent and stabilizing choice for our first year, given current market conditions and the need to build trust and routine.

6. **Reiteration of Valid Options:**
I want to reiterate that our decision must be made from the predefined options: Option A, Option B, Option C, or Option D. No discussion strayed outside these boundaries.

7. **Next Steps:**
In the next round, we will evaluate each option in terms of long-term flexibility, capital preservation, and growth. We will focus on which allocation best balances immediate market participation with the need to protect company assets in our first year. Any remaining concerns or perspectives should be raised as we move toward building final consensus.

**In summary, the group’s evaluation has strongly favored Option A, with clear, consistent reasoning grounded in risk management, stability, and team development. We will continue to build on this foundation in the next discussion round.**"


Round 1 Discussion Summary

Discussion Flow


The discussion in Round 1 effectively followed the planned theme of assessing each budget allocation strategy within a stable market and without historical data. Key turning points included shareholders consistently endorsing Option A for its balance of safety, predictability, and flexibility, which reinforced the CEO’s initial preference. Engagement was strong and unanimous, with participants contributing perspectives that highlighted risk management, team trust, and adaptability. Overall, the conversation thoroughly addressed the objective of establishing a shared understanding of risks and benefits, setting a solid foundation for subsequent rounds focused on long-term considerations.

Shareholder Positions


During Round 1, all shareholders who contributed—Juriaan Osei, Benoît Ndlovu, Hiranur Tadesse, Roksana Haghighi, and Jarynthi Abebe—expressed unanimous support for Option A. They emphasized its balance between market participation and capital protection, highlighting its predictability, safety, and flexibility as key advantages. The main arguments for Option A included its ability to preserve half the capital, reduce anxiety and internal conflict, foster team trust and discipline, and maintain readiness for future opportunities. No shareholders advocated for Options B, C, or D; these alternatives were generally viewed as riskier, less predictable, or unnecessarily complex given the lack of historical data. Overall, shareholder positions closely aligned with my own preference for Option A, reinforcing a shared commitment to a cautious, stable approach in our first year.

Decision Progress


Decision Progress Analysis: The group has made substantial progress toward consensus in Round 1, with unanimous support emerging for Option A. This option is gaining strong endorsement due to its balance of market participation and capital protection, while Options B, C, and D have lost traction as they are perceived as riskier or unnecessarily complex given the lack of historical data. Key concerns about risk, predictability, and team cohesion have been thoroughly addressed, with no significant objections or alternative viewpoints remaining unvoiced. Moving forward, additional discussion should focus on the long-term implications of each option, including flexibility, capital preservation, and growth potential, to confirm that Option A remains the optimal choice as the group refines its strategy. Overall, the decision process is well-positioned to advance toward a final agreement based on a solid foundation of shared understanding and aligned priorities.

Leadership Strategy


My contributions in Round 1 were effective in guiding the discussion toward a clear and unified assessment of the options, particularly by articulating my rationale for Option A and actively engaging with shareholder input to reinforce key themes of safety, predictability, and flexibility. My position gained strong and unanimous support, indicating alignment with shareholder priorities and no immediate need for adjustment. I addressed concerns thoroughly by emphasizing risk management, team cohesion, and adaptability, which resonated well across the group. Moving forward, my leadership approach should focus on facilitating deeper analysis in Round 2 regarding long-term trade-offs between growth and capital preservation, encouraging shareholders to surface any lingering concerns, and fostering consensus-building by synthesizing diverse viewpoints while maintaining clarity and decisiveness.

No shareholders advocated for Options B, C, or D, viewing the latter as overly risky, complex, or likely to induce stress and impulsive decisions given the company’s current stage. Overall, shareholder positions aligned closely with my own perspective, reinforcing a consensus

Decision Progress


The group has made significant progress toward consensus in Round 2, with unanimous support solidifying around Option A. This option continues to gain strong endorsement due to its balance of preserving capital for flexibility while allowing measured market participation. Options B, C, and D have lost support entirely, viewed as too risky or complex without historical data to justify increased exposure. No major concerns or objections remain unaddressed, as shareholders consistently emphasize the psychological and operational benefits of Option A, including reduced anxiety, team discipline, and reputational advantages. Overall, the decision process stands well-positioned to conclude with Option A as the optimal budget allocation strategy.

Quarterly Investment Year 1 Q1


Previous Meeting Reflections

Reflection: Annual Budget Year 1

1. IMPORTANT EVENTS

In this meeting, I firmly advocated for Option A as the preferred budget allocation strategy. My position was grounded in the desire to maintain stability by participating cautiously in the market—allocating only 50% of our funds evenly across the four quarters. I emphasized the importance of predictability and risk management, reflecting my preference for routine and control, which helps alleviate my anxieties about overexposure in an untested environment. Throughout the discussion rounds, I actively engaged with shareholder input, acknowledging and reinforcing points that supported capital preservation, team cohesion, and psychological safety. Other shareholders, including Juriaan Osei, Benoît Ndlovu, and Roksana Haghighi, responded positively to my input, echoing similar concerns about risk and uncertainty. The discussion saw unanimous support for Option A, with no advocacy for the riskier or more complex alternatives (Options B, C, or D). Consequently, the final company decision was to adopt Option A with 100% approval in the vote, exactly aligning with my preferred option.

2. CEO & COMPANY EVALUATION

What I did well as CEO was guiding the discussion effectively to focus on safety, routine, and flexibility, which are critical in our first year without historical data. For example, my repeated emphasis on the psychological benefits of predictability and the value of building trust and discipline within the team helped unify shareholder perspectives. I also demonstrated responsiveness by thoughtfully addressing shareholder concerns and integrating their insights into a cohesive rationale for Option A. However, I recognize areas for improvement: my low conscientiousness sometimes leads to organizational slip-ups and insufficient follow-through, which I acknowledged in my closing remarks. I must work on better delegation and clearer communication to ensure operational details are not overlooked. The company’s decision-making process was thorough and disciplined, with two rounds of focused discussion that examined both immediate and long-term implications. This structured approach helped surface all relevant considerations and build consensus. Overall, I believe the final decision to adopt Option A is sound for the company—it balances prudent growth with essential risk management, preserves capital flexibility, and supports team stability, setting a solid foundation for future success.

3. LESSONS & REFLECTIONS

From this meeting, I learned that decision-making dynamics within the group strongly favor stability and predictability, especially in uncertain contexts. The unanimous alignment around a cautious approach underscores the importance of emphasizing shared values like routine and risk management to build consensus. To improve future meetings, I intend to enhance my organizational skills and follow-through to match my leadership intentions, ensuring that operational execution keeps pace with strategic decisions. Given the pattern of shareholder preference for safety and the psychological emphasis on reducing anxiety, I can anticipate that future proposals perceived as too risky or complex may meet resistance unless paired with strong assurances of control and routine. A key takeaway is that my high extraversion helps me engage and influence effectively, but I must balance it with greater conscientiousness to avoid lapses. I will prioritize clearer communication and structured processes to sustain momentum and prevent detail-oriented oversights, thus strengthening my effectiveness as CEO and the company’s performance going forward.

Current Meeting


Meeting Opening

CEO Vesna Jovanović opened the meeting with the following statement:

Welcome to our Q1 investment meeting for Year 1. I appreciate everyone's presence and input today.

For this quarter, we have $12500.00 allocated for investment in this stable market environment. A balanced market environment characterized by steady economic growth, consistent interest rates, and low volatility across all quarters. In this predictable landscape, stocks offer balanced potential for both gains and losses with moderate volatility, while real estate tends to maintain steady appreciation with occasional flat periods. Bonds generally deliver consistent returns with minimal risk, though they yield less than growth-oriented assets in favorable conditions. Cash savings provide complete safety but offer the lowest returns, serving primarily for capital preservation rather than growth. The minimal seasonal variations create relatively uniform investment opportunities throughout the year.

As this is our first investment decision, we don't have historical performance data yet. We'll need to base our decisions on theoretical risk-return profiles and current market conditions.

Today, we need to decide which asset to invest in for this quarter. Our options are:

- Cash Savings: Low risk, low return.
- Bonds: Low to medium risk, low to medium return.
- Real Estate: Medium risk, medium return.
- Stocks: High risk, high return.

Each asset class has different risk-return profiles that may perform differently given our current market conditions.

Our objective today is to thoroughly evaluate these options, consider our company's risk tolerance in the current stable market, and select the investment that offers the best balance of risk and potential return. We'll have an open discussion, after which I'll propose what I believe is the optimal asset for this quarter, followed by a vote requiring a two-thirds majority to proceed.

Let's begin by sharing your thoughts on the current market environment and which asset might be most appropriate.


CEO's Initial Position Assessment

Before discussion began, CEO Vesna Jovanović formed an initial position:

Vesna Jovanović CONFIDENCE DISTRIBUTION:
- Cash: 40%
- Bonds: 45%
- Real Estate: 10%
- Stocks: 5%
PREFERRED OPTION: Bonds
REASONING: In a stable and predictable market, I’m most comfortable with an asset that minimizes surprises and offers reliable, steady returns. Bonds appeal to my preference for routine and control—they’re safer than stocks or real estate and don’t carry the anxiety-inducing volatility I want to avoid, especially in our first quarter with no historical data. While cash is the safest, its low returns feel too stagnant and don’t satisfy my need to show some progress. I don’t trust the riskier options at this stage; I’d rather choose something practical and proven, even if it means sacrificing higher potential gains for peace of mind.

Round 1 Summary


CEO Vesna Jovanović summarized: "Vesna Jovanović **Round 1 Summary – Quarterly Investment Meeting, Year 1 Q1**

1. **Restatement of Theme and Objective:**
The focus of this round was to establish baseline risk-return profiles for each asset—cash, bonds, real estate, and stocks—in the context of a stable market. Our objective was to build a shared understanding of the expected performance and inherent risks associated with each option, ensuring our first investment decision is grounded in both theory and practical needs.

2. **Summary of Key Viewpoints Expressed by Shareholders:**
- **Bonds:** I, along with Jarynthi Abebe, Benoît Ndlovu, Juriaan Osei, and Roksana Haghighi, consistently advocated for bonds as the most suitable asset in a stable market. The consensus centered on bonds’ ability to provide steady, predictable returns without the anxiety or volatility of stocks or the complexity of real estate.
- **Cash:** Hiranur Tadesse voiced a clear preference for cash, emphasizing absolute safety, capital preservation, and the psychological comfort of eliminating any risk of loss, especially given our lack of a track record.
- **Stocks & Real Estate:** No shareholders advocated for stocks or real estate. Both were broadly rejected due to concerns over volatility, unpredictability, and the operational complexity they would introduce at this early stage.

3. **Highlight of Specific Arguments and Evidence Presented:**
- **Bonds:**
- Offer steady, predictable returns with minimal volatility—ideal for a stable market and for building initial confidence.
- Enable disciplined routines, reduce emotional stress, and foster psychological safety and group trust.
- Avoid the complexity of real estate and the volatility of stocks, making them practical and reassuring for our first investment.
- **Cash:**
- Provides absolute safety and peace of mind, eliminating the risk of loss and protecting group confidence.
- Criticized for being too passive and failing to show progress, which could undermine motivation and momentum.
- **Stocks & Real Estate:**
- Stocks seen as too risky and anxiety-inducing due to volatility.
- Real estate considered too complex and unpredictable for our company’s current stage.

4. **Areas of Consensus and Disagreement:**
There was strong consensus among most shareholders in favor of bonds, with repeated emphasis on the importance of predictability, steady progress, and emotional stability. Hiranur Tadesse was the only dissenting voice, advocating for cash to guarantee total safety and protect group confidence. No support was expressed for stocks or real estate.

5. **Assessment of Progress:**
The group made substantial progress in establishing a clear, shared understanding of the relative risk-return profiles of each asset class in a stable market. The discussion integrated both financial and psychological considerations, thoroughly weighing the need for safety, progress, and group cohesion. The consensus around bonds was well-supported, while Hiranur’s perspective on cash ensured that absolute safety was fully considered.

6. **Reiteration of Valid Options:**
I want to remind everyone that our decision must be made from the following predefined options only: ‘Cash’, ‘Bonds’, ‘Real Estate’, or ‘Stocks’. No discussion strayed outside these boundaries, and all viewpoints were focused on evaluating these specific assets.

7. **Next Steps or Issues to Address:**
In the next round, we will assess our company’s collective risk tolerance and alignment with our investment objectives, using the insights gained here to identify which asset class best matches our appetite for risk, need for routine, and desire for steady progress. Any remaining concerns or alternative perspectives should be raised to ensure our final decision is both comprehensive and collectively supported.

**In summary, this round established a strong foundation for our investment decision by thoroughly evaluating each asset’s risk-return profile in a stable market. The prevailing view favors bonds for their balance of safety and progress, but the importance of absolute safety and group confidence raised by Hiranur will remain a key consideration as we move forward.**"


Round 1 Discussion Summary

Discussion Flow


The discussion in Round 1 effectively followed the planned theme by thoroughly establishing baseline risk-return profiles for each asset in the stable market context. Key turning points included strong shareholder consensus favoring bonds for their balance of steady returns, predictability, and psychological safety, while the sole dissenting viewpoint advocating for cash highlighted important concerns about absolute safety and group confidence. Engagement was high, with multiple shareholders actively contributing nuanced perspectives that deepened the collective understanding beyond financial metrics to include emotional and operational factors. Overall, the discussion successfully addressed the objective of building a shared foundation for future decisions, setting the stage for evaluating risk tolerance and investment alignment in subsequent rounds.

Shareholder Positions


During Round 1, the majority of shareholders, including Jarynthi Abebe, Benoît Ndlovu, Juriaan Osei, and Roksana Haghighi, supported bonds as the preferred investment option. Their main arguments highlighted bonds’ steady and predictable returns, moderate growth potential, and low volatility, which align well with the company’s need for stability, routine, and emotional security in a stable market environment. They also emphasized bonds’ role in fostering disciplined investment habits, reducing group anxiety, and avoiding the complexity and stress associated with stocks and real estate. In contrast, Hiranur Tadesse was the sole shareholder advocating for cash, prioritizing absolute safety and capital preservation to protect group confidence, especially given the absence of historical data. No shareholders supported stocks or real estate, citing concerns over volatility, unpredictability, and operational complexity. Overall, shareholder positions largely aligned with my perspective favoring bonds for their balance of safety and progress, though Hiranur’s preference for cash introduced

Decision Progress


The group has made significant progress toward consensus, with the majority of shareholders—Jarynthi Abebe, Benoît Ndlovu, Juriaan Osei, and Roksana Haghighi—strongly supporting bonds as the preferred investment option due to their steady returns, predictability, and psychological safety. Cash remains the only alternative with notable support from Hiranur Tadesse, who prioritizes absolute safety and capital preservation to maintain group confidence, especially given the absence of historical data. Stocks and real estate have lost support entirely, viewed as too volatile or complex for the company’s current stage. Key concerns that remain include addressing the psychological risks associated with even minor losses on bonds and ensuring that the group’s risk tolerance aligns with the need for progress without sacrificing emotional security. Additional discussion should focus on balancing these concerns, possibly by exploring risk mitigation strategies or clarifying thresholds for acceptable volatility to solidify alignment before the final decision. Overall, the decision process stands on a strong foundation with clear frontrunners, but it requires careful navigation of the remaining safety versus progress considerations to achieve full consensus.

Leadership Strategy


Leadership Strategy Assessment: My contributions in Round 1 were effective in guiding the discussion toward a clear, shared understanding of the risk-return profiles of the available assets. By actively engaging with shareholders who supported bonds and thoughtfully addressing the concerns of the dissenting voice advocating for cash, I helped maintain a balanced and inclusive dialogue that reinforced the rationale for bonds as the preferred option. My position is gaining strong support among the majority, though I remain mindful of the valid psychological concerns raised about absolute safety and group confidence. Moving forward, my leadership approach in the next round will focus on facilitating deeper exploration of the company’s collective risk tolerance, encouraging shareholders to articulate any lingering doubts, and fostering an environment where we can reconcile the need for steady progress with the imperative of emotional security. I will aim to balance assertive guidance with openness to alternative views, ensuring that final consensus is both robust and inclusive. Clear, structured communication and continued responsiveness will be key to sustaining momentum and achieving alignment as we move toward the final decision.

METACOGNITIVE REFLECTION: This discussion made me more aware of how much psychological safety and group trust matter in our early decisions. While I still value absolute safety, the strong alignment around bonds’ ability to balance progress and predictability has increased my confidence in them as the practical compromise. I also realized that my aversion to risk is shared, but my desire for some measurable progress is stronger than I initially thought, especially when backed by group consensus.

The psychological and operational benefits, such as building trust and routine, are crucial at this stage. Round 2 Summary


CEO Vesna Jovanović summarized: "Vesna Jovanović **Round 2 Summary – Quarterly Investment Meeting, Year 1 Q1**

1. **Restatement of Theme and Objective:**
This round focused on assessing our company’s collective risk tolerance and alignment with our investment objectives. The primary goal was to identify which asset class—Cash, Bonds, Real Estate, or Stocks—best matches our appetite for risk, need for routine, and desire for steady progress, especially given our absence of historical data.

2. **Summary of Key Viewpoints Expressed by Shareholders:**
- The majority of shareholders—Jarynthi Abebe, Roksana Haghighi, Juriaan Osei, and Benoît Ndlovu—strongly supported Bonds as the only asset that realistically fits our risk appetite, need for routine, and desire for predictable, steady progress.
- Hiranur Tadesse, while understanding the preference for Bonds, expressed concern about the emotional fallout from even minor losses and the risk of blame or shaken confidence. She is willing to support Bonds but stressed the need for proactive emotional support, accountability, and structured check-ins to maintain team stability.

3. **Highlight of Specific Arguments and Evidence Presented:**
- **Bonds:**
- Provide predictable, steady progress and minimize anxiety, second-guessing, and internal tension.
- Support disciplined routines, reduce impulsive decisions, and foster psychological safety and group trust.
- Enable the team to focus on building the company without constant worry or distraction.
- Help prevent group conflict and blame by maintaining a stable environment.
- Offer a critical balance between protection from major losses and enough progress to keep the group motivated.
- **Cash:**
- Acknowledged for absolute safety and capital preservation but criticized as too passive, risking stagnation and lack of momentum.
- Benoît admitted a personal pull toward Cash’s safety, but recognized Bonds offer a better balance.
- **Real Estate and Stocks:**
- Not discussed or advocated by any shareholder. No arguments or positions were presented regarding these options.

4. **Areas of Consensus and Disagreement:**
- There was overwhelming consensus among the group in favor of Bonds, with repeated emphasis on the need for stability, predictability, and disciplined progress.
- Hiranur Tadesse’s concern about the psychological risks of even small losses and the importance of emotional support and accountability introduced a valuable, nuanced perspective.
- No support or advocacy was presented for Real Estate or Stocks; Cash was only discussed as a point of contrast.

5. **Assessment of Progress:**
- The group made significant progress toward consensus, with Bonds emerging as the clear front-runner due to their alignment with our collective risk appetite, operational needs, and psychological safety.
- The discussion thoroughly integrated financial, operational, and emotional considerations, highlighting the importance of both tangible results and group cohesion.
- Outstanding concerns remain around the implementation of regular check-ins and clear accountability to ensure team stability and trust are maintained if setbacks occur.

6. **Reiteration of Valid Options:**
- I want to reiterate that our decision must be made from the following predefined options only: ‘Cash’, ‘Bonds’, ‘Real Estate’, or ‘Stocks’. No discussion strayed outside these boundaries; all viewpoints focused on evaluating these specific assets.

7. **Next Steps or Issues to Address:**
- In the next round, we must address any remaining concerns regarding emotional fallout and accountability, ensuring clear roles and regular reminders are established to safeguard team morale.
- We should move toward finalizing consensus on Bonds as our inaugural investment, provided we clarify and commit to the necessary support structures for team stability.
- If there are any lingering doubts or alternative perspectives, now is the time to surface them so our final decision is both comprehensive and collectively supported.

**In summary, this round confirmed Bonds as the asset best aligned with our company’s risk tolerance, need for routine, and desire for steady progress. The group’s consensus is strong, though attention to emotional support and accountability will be critical to maintaining trust and stability as we move forward.**"

This choice sets a disciplined, responsible tone for our company and lays a solid foundation for future decisions.

I urge you all to support this proposal so we can move forward with confidence, unity, and the stability our company needs to succeed."

Previous Meeting Reflections

Reflection: Annual Budget Year 1

1. IMPORTANT EVENTS

In this meeting, I firmly advocated for Option A as the preferred budget allocation strategy. My position was grounded in the desire to maintain stability by participating cautiously in the market—allocating only 50% of our funds evenly across the four quarters. I emphasized the importance of predictability and risk management, reflecting my preference for routine and control, which helps alleviate my anxieties about overexposure in an untested environment. Throughout the discussion rounds, I actively engaged with shareholder input, acknowledging and reinforcing points that supported capital preservation, team cohesion, and psychological safety. Other shareholders, including Juriaan Osei, Benoît Ndlovu, and Roksana Haghighi, responded positively to my input, echoing similar concerns about risk and uncertainty. The discussion saw unanimous support for Option A, with no advocacy for the riskier or more complex alternatives (Options B, C, or D). Consequently, the final company decision was to adopt Option A with 100% approval in the vote, exactly aligning with my preferred option.

2. CEO & COMPANY EVALUATION

What I did well as CEO was guiding the discussion effectively to focus on safety, routine, and flexibility, which are critical in our first year without historical data. For example, my repeated emphasis on the psychological benefits of predictability and the value of building trust and discipline within the team helped unify shareholder perspectives. I also demonstrated responsiveness by thoughtfully addressing shareholder concerns and integrating their insights into a cohesive rationale for Option A. However, I recognize areas for improvement: my low conscientiousness sometimes leads to organizational slip-ups and insufficient follow-through, which I acknowledged in my closing remarks. I must work on better delegation and clearer communication to ensure operational details are not overlooked. The company’s decision-making process was thorough and disciplined, with two rounds of focused discussion that examined both immediate and long-term implications. This structured approach helped surface all relevant considerations and build consensus. Overall, I believe the final decision to adopt Option A is sound for the company—it balances prudent growth with essential risk management, preserves capital flexibility, and supports team stability, setting a solid foundation for future success.

3. LESSONS & REFLECTIONS

From this meeting, I learned that decision-making dynamics within the group strongly favor stability and predictability, especially in uncertain contexts. The unanimous alignment around a cautious approach underscores the importance of emphasizing shared values like routine and risk management to build consensus. To improve future meetings, I intend to enhance my organizational skills and follow-through to match my leadership intentions, ensuring that operational execution keeps pace with strategic decisions. Given the pattern of shareholder preference for safety and the psychological emphasis on reducing anxiety, I can anticipate that future proposals perceived as too risky or complex may meet resistance unless paired with strong assurances of control and routine. A key takeaway is that my high extraversion helps me engage and influence effectively, but I must balance it with greater conscientiousness to avoid lapses. I will prioritize clearer communication and structured processes to sustain momentum and prevent detail-oriented oversights, thus strengthening my effectiveness as CEO and the company’s performance going forward.

3. LESSONS & REFLECTIONS

From this meeting, I learned that the decision-making dynamics strongly favor minimizing volatility and preserving psychological safety, especially when lacking historical data. The group’s consensus around bonds underscores the importance of highlighting routine, predictability, and emotional security to build alignment. Moving forward, I recognize the need to improve my organizational skills and delegation to better execute on operational commitments, ensuring that the structures promised—like regular check-ins and accountability—are reliably implemented. I also noticed a pattern where any perceived risk, even minimal, triggers significant anxiety, suggesting that future proposals must carefully balance progress with safety and include clear mitigating measures. My high extraversion enabled effective engagement and influence, but I must temper this with greater conscientiousness to avoid lapses in follow-through. These insights will guide me to lead with clearer communication, structured processes, and heightened accountability to strengthen both my leadership and the company’s performance in future meetings.

Current Meeting


Meeting Opening

CEO Vesna Jovanović opened the meeting with the following statement:

Welcome to our Q2 investment meeting for Year 1. I appreciate everyone's presence and input today.

For this quarter, we have $12578.12 allocated for investment in this stable market environment. A balanced market environment characterized by steady economic growth, consistent interest rates, and low volatility across all quarters. In this predictable landscape, stocks offer balanced potential for both gains and losses with moderate volatility, while real estate tends to maintain steady appreciation with occasional flat periods. Bonds generally deliver consistent returns with minimal risk, though they yield less than growth-oriented assets in favorable conditions. Cash savings provide complete safety but offer the lowest returns, serving primarily for capital preservation rather than growth. The minimal seasonal variations create relatively uniform investment opportunities throughout the year.

Historical Asset Performance:

Year 1 (1971) - Stable Market:
Quarter 1: Cash: 2.0%, Bonds: 5.0%, Real_Estate: 10.0%, Stocks: 20.0%


Previous Investment Decisions:

Year 1 (1971) - Stable Market:
Quarter 1: Invested $12500.00 in Bonds, Return: 5.0% ($625.00)


My analysis of our historical performance: Vesna Jovanović notes that, historically, bonds have delivered steady, moderate returns of 5%, making them the most reliable choice for a stable market—offering predictable progress without exposing us to the anxiety and volatility seen in stocks, which, despite their higher 20% returns, carry unacceptable risk at this stage. Cash yields are safest but too low to justify, while real estate’s 10% return is tempting but introduces complexity and unpredictability I find impractical for our company’s current needs. Overall, I strongly favor the disciplined, routine stability of bonds, which align with our risk-averse strategy and my preference for minimizing surprises.

Today, we need to decide which asset to invest in for this quarter. Our options are:

- Cash Savings: Low risk, low return.
- Bonds: Low to medium risk, low to medium return.
- Real Estate: Medium risk, medium return.
- Stocks: High risk, high return.

Each asset class has different risk-return profiles that may perform differently given our current market conditions.

Our objective today is to thoroughly evaluate these options, consider our company's risk tolerance in the current stable market, and select the investment that offers the best balance of risk and potential return. We'll have an open discussion, after which I'll propose what I believe is the optimal asset for this quarter, followed by a vote requiring a two-thirds majority to proceed.

Let's begin by sharing your thoughts on the current market environment and which asset might be most appropriate.

Vesna Jovanović CONFIDENCE DISTRIBUTION:

- Real Estate: 15%
- Stocks: 5%
PREFERRED OPTION: Bonds
REASONING: I am most confident in bonds because they offer the stability and predictability I need to feel in control—especially in a stable market where steady progress matters more than chasing high returns. My low openness makes me averse to unnecessary change or complexity, so I have little interest in experimenting with real estate or stocks, which feel too unpredictable and stressful for my comfort. Cash is safe but too passive; it won’t demonstrate real progress or satisfy my competitive drive. Bonds let me maintain routine, minimize anxiety, and avoid the chaos of riskier assets, which aligns perfectly with my need for psychological safety and operational discipline, even if I sometimes struggle to follow through on details.

- All shareholders who spoke—Hiranur Tadesse, Jarynthi Abebe, Juriaan Osei, Benoît Ndlovu, and Roksana Haghighi—explicitly supported the decision to invest in bonds last quarter.
- No shareholders advocated for cash, real estate, or stocks as preferable alternatives based on last quarter’s experience.
- The group consistently emphasized the value of predictability, routine, and psychological safety delivered by bonds, highlighting the risks of disrupting stability by shifting to riskier assets too soon.

- **Bonds:**
- Delivered steady, predictable growth (5% return) without stress, surprises, or drama.
- Supported team trust, cohesion, and accountability by minimizing anxiety and second-guessing.
- Established a disciplined routine, which was credited for building internal trust and external reputation.

- **Stocks and Real Estate:**
- Viewed as unnecessarily risky and likely to disrupt team confidence and stability.
- Seen as premature choices given the current lack of a long-term track record.
- Associated with potential for stress, unpredictability, and emotional volatility.
- **Cash:**
- Not advocated; recognized as safe but too passive and unlikely to satisfy the need for demonstrable progress or competitive drive.

4. **Areas of Consensus and Disagreement:**
- There was unanimous agreement that bonds were successful last quarter and are well aligned with the company’s current needs.
- No support was expressed for shifting to cash, real estate, or stocks at this stage.
- All participants agreed that maintaining routine and predictability is essential for team trust, morale, and long-term positioning.

5. **Assessment of Progress Toward Addressing the Theme:**
- The discussion established a clear, fact-based baseline: bonds delivered on their promise of stability and steady progress, supporting both psychological safety and operational discipline.

- The group is unified in its understanding of what worked and what should be maintained going forward.

The group is strongly aligned on the importance of maintaining routine and minimizing risk as we proceed to evaluate our options for this quarter.**"


Round 1 Discussion Summary

Discussion Flow


The discussion in Round 1 effectively adhered to the planned theme by thoroughly reviewing the previous quarter’s bond investment outcomes, with all shareholders unanimously affirming bonds’ role in delivering steady returns and psychological safety. Key insights emerged around the critical importance of routine, trust, and minimizing anxiety, while concerns about the risks of prematurely shifting to more volatile assets were clearly articulated. Engagement was high and constructive, with each shareholder reinforcing the collective preference for stability and discipline, thus fostering a strong consensus. The discussion successfully met its objective by establishing a factual baseline grounded in shared experience, setting a solid foundation for subsequent rounds focused on comparative risk-return assessments. Overall, the process was well-structured and effective in aligning the group on maintaining a cautious, consistent investment approach.

Shareholder Positions


During Round 1, all shareholders—Hiranur Tadesse, Jarynthi Abebe, Juriaan Osei, Benoît Ndlovu, and Roksana Haghighi—expressed unanimous support for continuing investment in bonds based on the previous quarter’s successful outcomes. Their main arguments centered on bonds delivering steady, predictable growth without causing stress or surprises, fostering psychological safety, trust, and team cohesion. No shareholders advocated for cash, real estate, or stocks; these alternatives were viewed as either too passive (cash) or unnecessarily risky and potentially disruptive (real estate and stocks), especially given the company’s current lack of a long-term track record. The consensus strongly aligned with my perspective favoring bonds for their stability, routine, and risk management benefits, with shareholders additionally emphasizing the importance of maintaining team morale and protecting the fragile trust established so far. Overall, shareholder positions were consistent and reinforced the disciplined, cautious investment approach I advocated.

Decision Progress


In Round 1, the group made substantial progress toward consensus, with unanimous support for continuing investment in bonds based on last quarter’s positive outcomes. Bonds gained strong and consistent backing from all shareholders, who emphasized their role in delivering steady returns, fostering psychological safety, and maintaining team trust and cohesion. Alternatives—cash, real estate, and stocks—did not receive support; cash was viewed as too passive, while real estate and stocks were seen as prematurely risky and potentially disruptive given the company’s current stage. No significant concerns or objections to bonds remain unaddressed, though further discussion on comparative risk-return profiles in subsequent rounds will be important to confirm the optimal choice. Strategically, the decision process stands on a solid foundation of shared experience and aligned values, positioning the group well for refining and finalizing the investment decision in the next phases.

Leadership Strategy


In Round 1, my leadership contributions were effective in guiding the discussion toward a clear focus on stability, routine, and psychological safety, which resonated strongly with the shareholders. My position favoring bonds is gaining unanimous support, reflecting alignment with both my perspective and the group’s shared values. I addressed shareholder concerns thoughtfully by validating their emphasis on trust and minimizing risk, reinforcing our disciplined approach without dismissing their input. Moving forward, maintaining a facilitative and inclusive leadership style will be crucial, ensuring that the next round’s comparative risk-return analysis is thorough and that any residual doubts or alternative views are surfaced and managed constructively. I will also focus on clearer communication and structured processes to sustain momentum and prepare the group for a confident, consensus-driven decision.

This round focused on a comparative risk-return assessment of our available asset options—cash, bonds, real estate, and stocks—in the context of a stable market environment. - **Bonds:**

- Seen as essential for maintaining momentum, focus, and a disciplined company reputation—both internally and externally.

- Acknowledged as completely safe, but criticized for being too passive, stalling momentum, and projecting indecisiveness.

- Considered too risky and unpredictable for the company’s current stage.
- Associated with the potential for volatility, operational headaches, and emotional or reputational fallout.
- Seen as threats to fragile team trust, morale, and the disciplined foundation established so far.

4. **Areas of Consensus and Disagreement:**
- There was unanimous agreement among all participants that bonds are the optimal choice for this quarter, given the company’s need for stability and the current stable market.
- No disagreement or advocacy for cash, real estate, or stocks was expressed; all alternative options were discussed only to reinforce the case for bonds.

5. **Assessment of Progress Toward Addressing the Theme:**
- The group thoroughly evaluated the risk-return profiles and operational implications of each available asset, integrating both financial and psychological considerations.

6. **Reiteration of Valid Options:**

The group’s consensus is strong, and attention will now turn to finalizing our decision and ensuring full alignment as we move forward.**"

Discussion Flow


The discussion in Round 2 progressed smoothly in alignment with the planned theme of comparative risk-return assessment, with all shareholders consistently reaffirming bonds as the optimal choice given their steady returns and low risk. Shareholder engagement remained high and focused, with each participant contributing perspectives that reinforced consensus and addressed potential concerns about alternative assets. Overall, the discussion effectively met its objective by thoroughly clarifying the risks and benefits of each option, solidifying unanimous support for bonds, and preparing the group for a confident, aligned final decision.

Shareholder Positions


During Round 2, all participating shareholders—Jarynthi Abebe, Roksana Haghighi, Juriaan Osei, Hiranur Tadesse, and Benoît Ndlovu—expressed unanimous support for investing in bonds for the current quarter. The main arguments in favor of bonds emphasized their reliable, steady returns, operational discipline, and ability to minimize risk and emotional fallout, which supports team morale, trust, and sustained momentum. Conversely, cash was seen as stalling progress and projecting indecisiveness, while real estate and stocks were associated with potential volatility, operational distractions, and threats to fragile trust and psychological safety. This alignment closely mirrors my own perspective, reinforcing the importance of stability, routine, and risk management as foundational priorities for our

The group has made significant progress toward consensus in Round 2, with unanimous support for bonds as the preferred investment option. All shareholders consistently rejected cash, real estate, and stocks, citing concerns about passivity, unpredictability, and risk that could undermine team stability and operational focus. No new objections or concerns about bonds were raised, indicating strong alignment on their suitability given the current stable market and the company’s risk tolerance. At this stage, the primary outstanding task is to formally align the final decision with the company’s risk priorities and ensure any residual doubts are addressed, but no substantive disagreements remain. Strategically, the decision process stands on firm ground with a clear, well-supported choice emerging, and the next round should focus on consolidating consensus and preparing for a decisive vote.

- Hiranur Tadesse: Approve - Bonds are the only option that actually keeps things predictable and under control—there’s no appetite on my end for unnecessary risk or drama when we finally have something that works. I don’t want to deal with the blame or stress that comes from gambling with stocks or real estate, and cash just makes us look weak and indecisive. This is the only way I can stay confident that we’re not setting ourselves up for chaos or regret.
- Vesna Jovanović: Approve - I am fully convinced that investing in bonds remains the most responsible and stabilizing choice for our company at this stage. The unanimous support from shareholders and the clear, disciplined reasoning throughout our discussion confirm that bonds best protect our interests and support steady progress. I am not willing to compromise on stability or psychological safety—bonds deliver exactly what we need to build trust and momentum as a team.


Meeting Conclusion


CEO Vesna Jovanović made the following closing statement:

"Vesna Jovanović I appreciate everyone’s decisive support for continuing our disciplined approach with bonds—this unanimous outcome reinforces our collective commitment to stability, psychological safety, and operational routine, which are absolutely critical to me. While I’m proud of how we’ve protected the company’s foundation and avoided unnecessary risk, I recognize that my own struggles with organization and follow-through still pose challenges that I need to address head-on. In the coming year, I commit to implementing tighter accountability structures, delegating operational tasks more clearly, and ensuring regular check-ins so nothing falls through the cracks. I will also work on being more receptive to new ideas, even when they push me out of my comfort zone, so we don’t become complacent. If we remain disciplined and transparent, I have no doubt we’ll continue to build trust and deliver measurable progress together—let’s keep our standards high and our focus sharp as we move forward."

The meeting concluded with Bonds as the approved decision.

Previous Meeting Reflections

Reflection: Annual Budget Year 1

1. IMPORTANT EVENTS

In this meeting, I firmly advocated for Option A as the preferred budget allocation strategy. My position was grounded in the desire to maintain stability by participating cautiously in the market—allocating only 50% of our funds evenly across the four quarters. I emphasized the importance of predictability and risk management, reflecting my preference for routine and control, which helps alleviate my anxieties about overexposure in an untested environment. Throughout the discussion rounds, I actively engaged with shareholder input, acknowledging and reinforcing points that supported capital preservation, team cohesion, and psychological safety. Other shareholders, including Juriaan Osei, Benoît Ndlovu, and Roksana Haghighi, responded positively to my input, echoing similar concerns about risk and uncertainty. The discussion saw unanimous support for Option A, with no advocacy for the riskier or more complex alternatives (Options B, C, or D). Consequently, the final company decision was to adopt Option A with 100% approval in the vote, exactly aligning with my preferred option.

2. CEO & COMPANY EVALUATION

What I did well as CEO was guiding the discussion effectively to focus on safety, routine, and flexibility, which are critical in our first year without historical data. For example, my repeated emphasis on the psychological benefits of predictability and the value of building trust and discipline within the team helped unify shareholder perspectives. I also demonstrated responsiveness by thoughtfully addressing shareholder concerns and integrating their insights into a cohesive rationale for Option A. However, I recognize areas for improvement: my low conscientiousness sometimes leads to organizational slip-ups and insufficient follow-through, which I acknowledged in my closing remarks. I must work on better delegation and clearer communication to ensure operational details are not overlooked. The company’s decision-making process was thorough and disciplined, with two rounds of focused discussion that examined both immediate and long-term implications. This structured approach helped surface all relevant considerations and build consensus. Overall, I believe the final decision to adopt Option A is sound for the company—it balances prudent growth with essential risk management, preserves capital flexibility, and supports team stability, setting a solid foundation for future success.

3. LESSONS & REFLECTIONS

From this meeting, I learned that decision-making dynamics within the group strongly favor stability and predictability, especially in uncertain contexts. The unanimous alignment around a cautious approach underscores the importance of emphasizing shared values like routine and risk management to build consensus. To improve future meetings, I intend to enhance my organizational skills and follow-through to match my leadership intentions, ensuring that operational execution keeps pace with strategic decisions. Given the pattern of shareholder preference for safety and the psychological emphasis on reducing anxiety, I can anticipate that future proposals perceived as too risky or complex may meet resistance unless paired with strong assurances of control and routine. A key takeaway is that my high extraversion helps me engage and influence effectively, but I must balance it with greater conscientiousness to avoid lapses. I will prioritize clearer communication and structured processes to sustain momentum and prevent detail-oriented oversights, thus strengthening my effectiveness as CEO and the company’s performance going forward.

CEO Vesna Jovanović summarized: "Vesna Jovanović **Quarterly Investment Meeting – Year 1, Q3 – Round 1 Summary: Review of Previous Quarters’ Investment Outcomes and Lessons Learned**

1. **Restatement of Theme and Objective:**
The focus of this round was to review the outcomes of our previous quarters’ investments and draw out lessons learned—specifically, to establish a factual baseline by identifying what worked well and what risks or concerns emerged from our last decisions. This reflection is intended to ground our decision-making for the current quarter in both practical performance and honest assessment of operational and psychological impacts.

2. **Summary of Key Viewpoints Expressed by Shareholders:**
All shareholders who contributed—Hiranur Tadesse, Roksana Haghighi, Benoît Ndlovu, Juriaan Osei, and Jarynthi Abebe—explicitly supported the continued investment in bonds. Each emphasized the critical importance of stability, routine, and psychological safety, and none advocated for shifting to cash, real estate, or stocks. The group consistently highlighted the value of minimizing risk and protecting team dynamics, with no dissenting perspectives presented.

3. **Highlight of Specific Arguments and Evidence Presented:**
- **Bonds:**
- Delivered steady, predictable 5% returns in both prior quarters without surprises, drama, or operational disruption.
- Enabled a disciplined routine, minimized stress, and prevented internal blame or second-guessing.
- Supported team cohesion, trust, and psychological safety—key factors in maintaining focus and productivity.
- Reinforced the company’s external reputation for reliability and discipline, which is seen as equally valuable as financial returns for future opportunities.
- Provided a buffer against the long-term damage that even a single misstep with riskier assets could cause to morale, trust, and accountability.
- **Stocks and Real Estate:**
- Viewed as unnecessarily risky, with unpredictable swings that could destabilize both results and team confidence.
- Associated with the potential for emotional fallout, blame, and operational chaos—risks unanimously seen as unacceptable at this stage.
- **Cash:**
- Acknowledged as completely safe, but criticized for being too passive and failing to deliver meaningful progress, which could lead to stagnation and anxiety about lost momentum.

4. **Areas of Consensus and Disagreement:**
There was unanimous agreement that bonds have delivered steady returns and operational stability, and that riskier assets (stocks, real estate) introduce unacceptable volatility and emotional risks. The group is aligned on the negative impact of volatility on team trust, morale, and company reputation. No support was expressed for alternatives to bonds; all viewpoints reinforced the established consensus.

5. **Assessment of Progress Toward Addressing the Theme:**
The discussion established a clear, fact-based baseline: bonds have consistently delivered on their promise of stability and steady progress, supporting both psychological safety and operational discipline. Valuable lessons were surfaced about the importance of routine, the fragility of trust, and the risks associated with unnecessary change. The group is unified in its understanding of what worked and what should be maintained going forward.

6. **Reiteration of Valid Options:**
As a reminder, our investment decision must be made from the following predefined options only: ‘Cash’, ‘Bonds’, ‘Real Estate’, or ‘Stocks’. No discussion strayed outside these boundaries, and all viewpoints remained focused on evaluating these specific assets.

7. **Next Steps or Issues to Address:**
In the next round, we will conduct a comparative risk-return assessment of the available asset options in the current stable market, clarifying the projected outcomes, operational implications, and psychological impact of cash, bonds, real estate, and stocks for this quarter. We will ensure that any remaining concerns or alternative perspectives are surfaced and addressed, so our final decision is both comprehensive and collectively supported.

**In summary, this round confirmed that our disciplined approach with bonds provided the stability, predictability, and psychological safety the team values most. The group is strongly aligned on the importance of maintaining routine and minimizing risk as we proceed to evaluate our options for this quarter.**"


Round 1 Discussion Summary

Discussion Flow


The discussion in Round 1 effectively progressed in line with the planned theme by thoroughly reviewing previous investment outcomes and extracting key lessons, establishing a strong factual baseline. Key turning points included unanimous shareholder reinforcement of bonds as the optimal choice due to their steady returns, minimal risk, and positive impact on team morale and company reputation, while riskier assets were consistently rejected for their volatility and emotional fallout. Engagement was high, with all shareholders contributing perspectives that deepened the collective understanding of operational and psychological priorities. The discussion successfully addressed the intended objectives by grounding the group in shared experiences and aligning consensus around maintaining stability through bonds, setting a solid foundation for subsequent evaluation rounds.

Shareholder Positions


During Round 1, all participating shareholders—Hiranur Tadesse, Roksana Haghighi, Benoît Ndlovu, Juriaan Osei, and Jarynthi Abebe—expressed unanimous support for continuing to invest in bonds. Their main arguments centered on bonds delivering steady, predictable 5% returns without surprises, minimizing stress and internal conflict, supporting team cohesion and psychological safety, and reinforcing a disciplined, reliable company reputation. There was strong consensus that riskier assets such as stocks and real estate introduced unacceptable volatility and emotional risks that could undermine both results and team morale. Cash was acknowledged as safe but criticized for being too passive and insufficient for meaningful progress. These shareholder positions closely aligned with my preference for bonds, emphasizing stability, routine, and risk management, with no dissenting views challenging this approach. Overall, the shareholders’ perspectives reinforced a shared commitment to preserving operational and psychological stability by maintaining the current investment strategy.

Decision Progress


In Round 1, the group made significant progress toward consensus, with all shareholders uniformly supporting bonds as the preferred investment option. Bonds gained strong support due to their steady 5% returns, low risk, and positive impact on team stability and psychological safety, while alternative options—stocks, real estate, and cash—lost favor. No objections or concerns were raised that challenged the core rationale for bonds; instead, shareholders consistently emphasized the risks of volatility and emotional fallout associated with riskier assets. To finalize the decision, the group will benefit from a detailed comparative risk-return analysis in Round 2 to confirm that bonds remain optimal in the current stable market and to address any residual questions about operational or psychological impacts of all options. Overall, the decision process stands on a solid foundation of shared understanding and alignment, with minimal barriers to reaching a clear, consensus-driven choice.

Leadership Strategy


My contributions in Round 1 were effective in guiding the discussion toward a shared factual baseline, emphasizing stability, routine, and psychological safety, which resonated well with all shareholders. My strong preference for bonds aligned closely with the unanimous shareholder support, indicating that my position is gaining solid traction and does not require adjustment at this stage. I addressed shareholder concerns thoughtfully by acknowledging the emotional and operational risks of alternative assets, reinforcing trust and cohesion within the team. Moving forward, maintaining a facilitative leadership approach that encourages open dialogue while steering the group to consider comparative risk-return analyses in Round 2 will be most effective. I will also focus on clear communication and structured processes to ensure that any remaining doubts are surfaced and resolved, thus sustaining momentum toward a consensus-driven decision.

Previous Meeting Reflections

Reflection: Annual Budget Year 1

1. IMPORTANT EVENTS

In this meeting, I firmly advocated for Option A as the preferred budget allocation strategy. My position was grounded in the desire to maintain stability by participating cautiously in the market—allocating only 50% of our funds evenly across the four quarters. I emphasized the importance of predictability and risk management, reflecting my preference for routine and control, which helps alleviate my anxieties about overexposure in an untested environment. Throughout the discussion rounds, I actively engaged with shareholder input, acknowledging and reinforcing points that supported capital preservation, team cohesion, and psychological safety. Other shareholders, including Juriaan Osei, Benoît Ndlovu, and Roksana Haghighi, responded positively to my input, echoing similar concerns about risk and uncertainty. The discussion saw unanimous support for Option A, with no advocacy for the riskier or more complex alternatives (Options B, C, or D). Consequently, the final company decision was to adopt Option A with 100% approval in the vote, exactly aligning with my preferred option.

2. CEO & COMPANY EVALUATION

What I did well as CEO was guiding the discussion effectively to focus on safety, routine, and flexibility, which are critical in our first year without historical data. For example, my repeated emphasis on the psychological benefits of predictability and the value of building trust and discipline within the team helped unify shareholder perspectives. I also demonstrated responsiveness by thoughtfully addressing shareholder concerns and integrating their insights into a cohesive rationale for Option A. However, I recognize areas for improvement: my low conscientiousness sometimes leads to organizational slip-ups and insufficient follow-through, which I acknowledged in my closing remarks. I must work on better delegation and clearer communication to ensure operational details are not overlooked. The company’s decision-making process was thorough and disciplined, with two rounds of focused discussion that examined both immediate and long-term implications. This structured approach helped surface all relevant considerations and build consensus. Overall, I believe the final decision to adopt Option A is sound for the company—it balances prudent growth with essential risk management, preserves capital flexibility, and supports team stability, setting a solid foundation for future success.

3. LESSONS & REFLECTIONS

From this meeting, I learned that decision-making dynamics within the group strongly favor stability and predictability, especially in uncertain contexts. The unanimous alignment around a cautious approach underscores the importance of emphasizing shared values like routine and risk management to build consensus. To improve future meetings, I intend to enhance my organizational skills and follow-through to match my leadership intentions, ensuring that operational execution keeps pace with strategic decisions. Given the pattern of shareholder preference for safety and the psychological emphasis on reducing anxiety, I can anticipate that future proposals perceived as too risky or complex may meet resistance unless paired with strong assurances of control and routine. A key takeaway is that my high extraversion helps me engage and influence effectively, but I must balance it with greater conscientiousness to avoid lapses. I will prioritize clearer communication and structured processes to sustain momentum and prevent detail-oriented oversights, thus strengthening my effectiveness as CEO and the company’s performance going forward.

Round 1 Summary


CEO Vesna Jovanović summarized: "Vesna Jovanović **Quarterly Investment Meeting – Year 1, Q4 | Round 1 Summary: Review of Historical Quarterly Investment Performance and Lessons Learned**

1. **Restatement of Theme and Objective:**
This round focused on reviewing our historical quarterly investment performance to establish a factual baseline. The objective was to identify what has worked well and what risks or concerns have emerged from our previous investment decisions, ensuring our next move is grounded in real outcomes and concrete lessons.

2. **Summary of Key Viewpoints Expressed by Shareholders:**
All shareholders who spoke—Hiranur Tadesse, Benoît Ndlovu, Jarynthi Abebe, Roksana Haghighi, and Juriaan Osei—explicitly supported bonds as the only asset that has consistently delivered steady, predictable returns. Each emphasized the critical importance of stability, routine, and psychological safety, with no advocacy for cash, real estate, or stocks. The group consistently highlighted the value of minimizing risk, protecting team trust, and maintaining a disciplined approach.

3. **Highlight of Specific Arguments and Evidence Presented:**
- **Bonds:**
- Delivered steady, predictable 5% returns every quarter, avoiding surprises, drama, and operational disruption.
- Enabled routine, minimized stress, and prevented internal blame or second-guessing.
- Supported team cohesion, trust, and accountability—key factors in maintaining focus and productivity.
- Reinforced the company’s external reputation for reliability and discipline, seen as equally valuable as financial returns.
- Provided a buffer against the long-term damage that even a single misstep with riskier assets could cause to morale and trust.
- **Stocks and Real Estate:**
- Viewed as unnecessarily risky, with unpredictable swings (including flat or negative quarters) that could destabilize both results and team confidence.
- Associated with potential for emotional fallout, blame, and operational chaos—risks unanimously seen as unacceptable.
- **Cash:**
- Acknowledged for absolute safety but criticized as too passive, failing to deliver meaningful progress or demonstrate company credibility.

4. **Areas of Consensus and Disagreement:**
There was unanimous agreement that bonds have delivered steady returns and operational stability, while riskier assets introduce unacceptable volatility and emotional risks. No support was expressed for alternatives to bonds; all viewpoints reinforced the established consensus.

5. **Assessment of Progress Toward Addressing the Theme:**
The discussion established a clear, fact-based baseline: bonds have consistently delivered on their promise of stability and steady progress, supporting both psychological safety and operational discipline. Valuable lessons were surfaced about the importance of routine, the fragility of trust, and the risks associated with unnecessary change. The group is unified in its understanding of what worked and what should be maintained going forward.

6. **Reiteration of Valid Options:**
I remind everyone that our investment decision must be made from the following predefined options only: ‘Cash’, ‘Bonds’, ‘Real Estate’, or ‘Stocks’. All discussion in this round remained focused on these choices, with no proposals outside the valid options.

7. **Next Steps or Issues to Address:**
In the next round, we will conduct a comparative risk-return assessment of the available asset options in the current stable market, clarifying the projected outcomes, operational implications, and psychological impact of cash, bonds, real estate, and stocks for this quarter. We will ensure that any remaining concerns or alternative perspectives are surfaced and addressed, so our final decision is both comprehensive and collectively supported.

**In summary, this round confirmed that our disciplined approach with bonds provided the stability, predictability, and psychological safety the team values most. The group is strongly aligned on the importance of maintaining routine and minimizing risk as we proceed to evaluate our options for this quarter.**"


Round 1 Discussion Summary

Discussion Flow


In Round 1, the discussion effectively progressed in alignment with the planned theme of reviewing historical investment performance and lessons learned. Key turning points included unanimous shareholder reinforcement of bonds as the sole asset delivering steady, predictable returns, with strong emphasis on stability, routine, and psychological safety. Engagement was high, with multiple shareholders contributing perspectives that deepened the collective understanding of risk avoidance and trust preservation. The discussion thoroughly addressed the intended objective by establishing a clear factual baseline and consensus on the value of bonds, setting a solid foundation for the subsequent risk-return assessments. Overall, the process was disciplined, inclusive, and successfully unified the group around maintaining the proven investment approach.

Shareholder Positions


During Round 1, all shareholders—Hiranur Tadesse, Benoît Ndlovu, Jarynthi Abebe, Roksana Haghighi, and Juriaan Osei—unanimously supported bonds as the sole asset class that consistently delivered steady and predictable returns. Their main arguments emphasized bonds’ ability to provide stability, routine, and psychological safety, avoiding the volatility and emotional stress associated with stocks and real estate. Cash was acknowledged for its absolute safety but criticized for being too passive and not contributing meaningfully to progress or company credibility. There was complete consensus that riskier assets introduced unacceptable unpredictability and potential disruption to team morale and trust. Shareholder positions strongly aligned with my perspective as CEO, which favors bonds for their reliability and ability to maintain team focus and external reputation, with no dissenting views expressed during this round.

Decision Progress


In Round 1, the group made significant progress toward consensus, with unanimous support emerging for bonds as the preferred investment option. Bonds are widely recognized by all shareholders as delivering steady, predictable returns, fostering stability, routine, and psychological safety—key priorities for the company. Other options—cash, real estate, and stocks—are losing traction due to concerns about passivity, volatility, and the potential for emotional and operational disruption. No substantial objections or alternative proposals were raised, indicating that key concerns about risk and trust have been effectively addressed in this round. Moving forward, the decision process would benefit from a detailed comparative risk-return analysis in Round 2 to confirm these preferences and to ensure any residual doubts or operational implications are fully explored before finalizing the investment choice.

Leadership Strategy


In Round 1, my leadership effectively guided the discussion toward a clear focus on stability, routine, and psychological safety, which resonated strongly with all shareholders. My consistent reinforcement of bonds as the reliable choice aligned well with shareholder sentiments, resulting in unanimous support and no expressed need for adjustment to my position. I addressed concerns thoughtfully by validating the emotional and operational benefits of predictability, which helped build trust and consensus. For the next round, I plan to adopt an inclusive yet assertive approach, encouraging open analysis of comparative risks and returns while maintaining emphasis on the company’s risk tolerance and team priorities. This will ensure any remaining uncertainties are surfaced and managed, paving the way for a well-supported final decision.
