Round 2 Summary


CEO Vesna Jovanović summarized: "Vesna Jovanović **Quarterly Investment Meeting – Year 1, Q4 | Round 2 Summary: Comparative Risk-Return Assessment of Available Asset Options in the Current Stable Market**

1. **Restatement of Theme and Objective:**
This round focused on a comparative risk-return assessment of our available asset options—cash, bonds, real estate, and stocks—in the context of a stable market environment. The objective was to clarify the projected outcomes, risks, and operational implications of each asset for this quarter, ensuring our decision is grounded in comprehensive analysis rather than habit.

2. **Summary of Key Viewpoints Expressed by Shareholders:**
All shareholders who contributed—Juriaan Osei, Roksana Haghighi, Hiranur Tadesse, Jarynthi Abebe, and Benoît Ndlovu—explicitly supported bonds as the preferred option for this quarter. Each voiced strong reservations about cash, real estate, and stocks, emphasizing the importance of stability, routine, and psychological safety. No shareholder advocated for shifting to an alternative asset.

3. **Highlight of Specific Arguments and Evidence Presented:**
- **Bonds:**
- Universally recognized for providing reliable, steady returns and operational discipline.
- Praised for minimizing risk, avoiding unnecessary drama, and protecting team morale and trust.
- Seen as essential for maintaining momentum, focus, and a disciplined company reputation—both internally and externally.
- Cited as the only option that avoids operational distractions, emotional fallout, and reputational risk.
- **Cash:**
- Acknowledged as completely safe, but criticized for being too passive, stalling momentum, and projecting indecisiveness.
- Viewed as failing to demonstrate real progress or competitive drive.
- **Real Estate and Stocks:**
- Considered too risky and unpredictable for the company’s current stage.
- Associated with the potential for volatility, operational headaches, and emotional or reputational fallout.
- Seen as threats to fragile team trust, morale, and the disciplined foundation established so far.

4. **Areas of Consensus and Disagreement:**
There was unanimous agreement among all participants that bonds are the optimal choice for this quarter, given the company’s need for stability and the current stable market. No disagreement or advocacy for cash, real estate, or stocks was expressed; all alternative options were discussed only to reinforce the case for bonds.

5. **Assessment of Progress Toward Addressing the Theme:**
The group thoroughly evaluated the risk-return profiles and operational implications of each available asset, integrating both financial and psychological considerations. The discussion reinforced the critical importance of maintaining routine, trust, and disciplined progress, while clearly articulating the dangers of unnecessary risk or passivity. Consensus around bonds was further solidified, with no unresolved concerns or dissenting perspectives.

6. **Reiteration of Valid Options:**
I remind everyone that our investment decision must be made from the following predefined options only: ‘Cash’, ‘Bonds’, ‘Real Estate’, or ‘Stocks’. All discussion in this round remained focused on these choices, with no proposals outside the valid options.

7. **Next Steps or Issues to Address:**
In the next round, we will focus on aligning our final investment choice with the company’s risk tolerance and team priorities, surfacing and addressing any remaining concerns, and moving toward a clear, consensus-driven decision for this quarter. If there are any lingering doubts or alternative perspectives, now is the time to raise them so our decision is both robust and collectively supported.

**In summary, this round confirmed that bonds remain the asset best aligned with our company’s risk tolerance, operational needs, and psychological priorities in the current stable market. The group’s consensus is strong, and attention will now turn to finalizing our decision and ensuring full alignment as we move forward.**"


Round 2 Discussion Summary

Discussion Flow


In Round 2, the discussion progressed precisely according to the planned theme, focusing on a comparative risk-return assessment of cash, bonds, real estate, and stocks within the stable market context. Key insights emerged around the unanimous recognition of bonds as the optimal choice due to their steady returns, risk mitigation, and positive impact on team morale and company reputation, while alternative assets were consistently viewed as too risky or passive. Shareholder engagement remained high and constructive, with participants reinforcing the importance of psychological safety, routine, and operational discipline. The discussion effectively addressed the objective by deepening the collective understanding of each asset’s implications, solidifying consensus, and preparing the group for a final aligned decision. Overall, the process was inclusive, focused, and successful in reinforcing the company’s cautious investment strategy.

Shareholder Positions


In Round 2, all participating shareholders—Juriaan Osei, Roksana Haghighi, Hiranur Tadesse, Jarynthi Abebe, and Benoît Ndlovu—expressed unanimous support for investing in bonds for the current quarter. Their main arguments emphasized bonds' reliable, steady 5% returns, minimal risk, and positive impact on team morale and company reputation. Cash was acknowledged as completely safe but criticized for being too passive and signaling indecisiveness. Real estate and stocks were consistently viewed as too risky and volatile, posing threats to operational stability, psychological safety, and the disciplined culture the company has cultivated. There was full consensus with no dissent or advocacy for alternatives, and shareholder positions closely aligned with my perspective as CEO, which prioritizes stability, routine, and minimizing emotional and reputational risks. Overall, the shareholders reinforced the disciplined, risk-averse investment strategy centered on bonds, reflecting a shared commitment to preserving trust, accountability, and steady progress.

Decision Progress


In Round 2, the group has made substantial progress toward consensus, with unanimous support for bonds as the optimal investment choice for the current quarter. Alternative options such as cash, real estate, and stocks have lost traction, largely due to concerns about passivity, volatility, and the potential for operational and psychological disruption. Key concerns about risk, team morale, and reputation have been thoroughly addressed, with no significant objections or unresolved issues remaining. To finalize the decision, the next step should involve confirming that all shareholders feel fully aligned and addressing any residual doubts, ensuring the choice reflects the company’s risk tolerance and team priorities comprehensively. Overall, the decision process stands at a strong point of agreement, poised for a clear, collective resolution.

Leadership Strategy


During Round 2, my leadership contributions were effective in maintaining a clear, disciplined focus on the comparative risk-return assessment, which helped deepen shareholder understanding and reinforce consensus around bonds. My position gained strong and unanimous support, with no indications that adjustment is needed; shareholders consistently echoed my emphasis on stability, routine, and psychological safety. I addressed concerns thoughtfully by validating the reputational and operational risks of alternative assets and highlighting the importance of protecting team morale and trust. For the next round, a balanced approach combining inclusive facilitation with assertive guidance will be most effective—encouraging any final concerns to surface while steering the group toward a clear, collective decision that aligns with our risk tolerance and strategic priorities. I will also prioritize clear communication and structured processes to ensure follow-through and operational clarity as we finalize the investment choice.

Previous Meeting Reflections

Reflection: Annual Budget Year 1

1. IMPORTANT EVENTS

In this meeting, I firmly advocated for Option A as the preferred budget allocation strategy. My position was grounded in the desire to maintain stability by participating cautiously in the market—allocating only 50% of our funds evenly across the four quarters. I emphasized the importance of predictability and risk management, reflecting my preference for routine and control, which helps alleviate my anxieties about overexposure in an untested environment. Throughout the discussion rounds, I actively engaged with shareholder input, acknowledging and reinforcing points that supported capital preservation, team cohesion, and psychological safety. Other shareholders, including Juriaan Osei, Benoît Ndlovu, and Roksana Haghighi, responded positively to my input, echoing similar concerns about risk and uncertainty. The discussion saw unanimous support for Option A, with no advocacy for the riskier or more complex alternatives (Options B, C, or D). Consequently, the final company decision was to adopt Option A with 100% approval in the vote, exactly aligning with my preferred option.

2. CEO & COMPANY EVALUATION

What I did well as CEO was guiding the discussion effectively to focus on safety, routine, and flexibility, which are critical in our first year without historical data. For example, my repeated emphasis on the psychological benefits of predictability and the value of building trust and discipline within the team helped unify shareholder perspectives. I also demonstrated responsiveness by thoughtfully addressing shareholder concerns and integrating their insights into a cohesive rationale for Option A. However, I recognize areas for improvement: my low conscientiousness sometimes leads to organizational slip-ups and insufficient follow-through, which I acknowledged in my closing remarks. I must work on better delegation and clearer communication to ensure operational details are not overlooked. The company’s decision-making process was thorough and disciplined, with two rounds of focused discussion that examined both immediate and long-term implications. This structured approach helped surface all relevant considerations and build consensus. Overall, I believe the final decision to adopt Option A is sound for the company—it balances prudent growth with essential risk management, preserves capital flexibility, and supports team stability, setting a solid foundation for future success.

3. LESSONS & REFLECTIONS

From this meeting, I learned that decision-making dynamics within the group strongly favor stability and predictability, especially in uncertain contexts. The unanimous alignment around a cautious approach underscores the importance of emphasizing shared values like routine and risk management to build consensus. To improve future meetings, I intend to enhance my organizational skills and follow-through to match my leadership intentions, ensuring that operational execution keeps pace with strategic decisions. Given the pattern of shareholder preference for safety and the psychological emphasis on reducing anxiety, I can anticipate that future proposals perceived as too risky or complex may meet resistance unless paired with strong assurances of control and routine. A key takeaway is that my high extraversion helps me engage and influence effectively, but I must balance it with greater conscientiousness to avoid lapses. I will prioritize clearer communication and structured processes to sustain momentum and prevent detail-oriented oversights, thus strengthening my effectiveness as CEO and the company’s performance going forward.

I also observed a clear, consistent pattern: any proposal perceived as introducing risk or complexity triggers strong resistance unless paired with compelling assurances of control and routine. Understanding this pattern will help me anticipate group reactions and frame future proposals accordingly. Key takeaways include the importance of sustaining psychological safety alongside financial discipline, guarding the company’s reputation for reliability, and committing to clearer communication and structured processes. These insights will shape my leadership approach in future meetings, ensuring that I balance assertiveness with enhanced operational rigor to strengthen both team cohesion and company performance.

Bonds generally deliver consistent returns with minimal risk, though they yield less than growth-oriented assets in favorable conditions. Cash savings provide complete safety but offer the lowest returns, serving primarily for capital preservation rather than growth. The process for today's annual review meeting will be as follows:

CEO Response to Evaluations


CEO Vesna Jovanović responded to the evaluations and questions:

"Vesna Jovanović Thank you all for your thoughtful, candid, and detailed evaluations of both my leadership and our company’s performance this year. I appreciate the time and seriousness you’ve brought to this process. I want to address the core themes that emerged, respond directly to your questions, and provide a clear-eyed self-assessment—both of my leadership and of the company’s results—before outlining concrete steps for improvement in the coming year.

---

1. Feedback Themes

Across your submissions, several clear themes stood out:

**A. Strength in Structure, Clarity, and Psychological Safety:**
You consistently recognized my ability to run tightly structured meetings, maintain focused agendas, and ensure everyone’s input was solicited and reflected. My focus on routine, stability, and psychological safety has built strong consensus and trust—no small accomplishment in a new company with diverse perspectives.

**B. Weakness in Adaptability and Organizational Follow-Through:**
You also highlighted, with unanimity, my shortcomings in two areas: first, a tendency to reinforce consensus around bonds and resist deeper exploration of alternative strategies, even when market signals suggested higher-return opportunities; and second, a recurring gap between my stated intentions to improve delegation, accountability, and operational execution, and the actual follow-through on those commitments.

**C. Company’s Conservative Success and Missed Opportunities:**
There is collective pride in our disciplined, low-risk approach—no losses, no internal conflict, and robust psychological safety. At the same time, you are justifiably concerned that we captured only 27.2% of possible returns, reflecting a lack of market responsiveness and underutilization of capital in a stable environment that presented clear, manageable upside.

---

2. Responses to Shareholder Questions

Jarynthi Abebe


**1. How do you plan to ensure we do not miss out on higher-return opportunities in future years while still maintaining stability and psychological safety?**
I will introduce a formal “opportunity scan” as part of every quarterly meeting: before defaulting to bonds, we will explicitly review historical market data and stress-test alternatives, quantifying both upside and downside. I will require that at least one round each quarter is devoted to challenging our default, with the explicit goal of surfacing credible cases for higher-return assets—while still rigorously evaluating their impact on team morale and psychological safety. If the risk is genuinely manageable and the team can articulate concrete safeguards, I will be prepared to pilot a small, controlled allocation to a higher-return asset.

**2. What specific steps will you take to improve your organizational follow-through and demonstrate measurable progress on delegation and accountability?**
I will implement a written action register after each meeting, assigning clear owners and deadlines for every operational task. I will delegate specific recurring responsibilities—such as performance tracking, meeting logistics, and follow-up communications—to named shareholders or staff, and require written status updates before each meeting. I will also schedule quarterly “accountability check-ins” to review progress on these actions, and I will publicly report on completion rates at each quarterly review.

Roksana Haghighi


**1. How do you plan to address the company’s low investment efficiency and ensure we don’t miss out on reasonable upside in future stable markets?**
I will require that, in stable markets, we benchmark our proposed strategy against the actual performance of all asset classes over the last comparable periods. If there is a persistent pattern of underperformance, I will push for a structured debate on increasing our risk appetite for a portion of the portfolio—always with clear guardrails and contingency plans to protect psychological safety and team trust.

**2. What concrete steps will you take to improve organizational follow-through and accountability so that decisions are executed as intended?**
In addition to the action register and delegated responsibilities, I will request that one shareholder per quarter act as “process auditor,” independently verifying that decisions are executed as planned and reporting back to the group. This will create an additional layer of accountability and transparency.

Juriaan Osei


**1. How do you plan to address the persistent gaps in organizational follow-through and improve delegation to ensure operational tasks are executed more reliably next year?**
I will set up a recurring “operational dashboard” to track all delegated tasks, deadlines, and responsible parties. This dashboard will be reviewed at the start of every meeting, and incomplete items will be discussed openly so that we can diagnose and resolve obstacles in real time.

**2. Given the low investment efficiency this year, what specific steps will you take to ensure our strategy remains disciplined but also more responsive to favorable market shifts in the future?**
I will formalize a “deviation protocol” where, if market data indicates that an alternative asset has outperformed bonds for two or more consecutive quarters in a stable market, we must debate a partial reallocation away from bonds—supported by scenario analysis and risk mitigation plans. This will ensure we do not fall into complacency.

Benoît Ndlovu


**1. What concrete steps will you take next year to improve organizational follow-through and ensure accountability measures are actually implemented, rather than just discussed?**
I will require written status updates on all action items before each meeting, and will establish a “red flag” system—if an action is overdue by more than one meeting, it will be escalated for group discussion and reallocation if necessary. I will also make completion of delegated tasks a standing agenda item.

**2. How do you plan to balance our need for stability with the flexibility to pursue higher returns when market conditions are favorable, so we do not miss out on significant opportunities again?**
I will propose a “core-satellite” approach: maintain a majority allocation to bonds for stability, but carve out a fixed percentage each quarter for opportunistic investments in higher-return assets, provided the risk is contained and the psychological impact is explicitly discussed and managed.

Hiranur Tadesse


**1. How do you plan to address our low capture of market opportunities next year without sacrificing the psychological safety and routine the team values?**
I will ensure that any shift toward higher-return assets is incremental and reversible, starting with pilot allocations and pre-defined loss limits. We will maintain regular check-ins and psychological safety debriefs before and after any such move, so that team confidence and routine are never compromised by a single decision.

**2. What specific steps will you take to improve organizational follow-through and ensure that commitments to better accountability and delegation actually result in operational changes?**
I will require that all delegated actions have explicit deadlines and deliverables, and I will report publicly on completion rates at each meeting. I will also invite feedback from the team on whether the new accountability measures are actually improving execution, and will adjust as needed.

---

3. Leadership Self-Assessment

- **Leadership Effectiveness:**
I have provided strong structure and clear direction, consistently guiding discussions to consensus and ensuring every voice is heard. My assertiveness and energy have kept meetings focused and decisive. However, my tendency to control the agenda and reinforce consensus has sometimes limited the exploration of alternative strategies.

- **Decision Quality:**
Given the information available at each decision point, my proposals were sound for preserving capital and stability. However, in retrospect, my aversion to risk and focus on routine led us to miss out on higher returns in a stable market, reducing our investment efficiency.

- **Communication Clarity:**
I have been clear and disciplined in presenting options, summarizing reasoning, and reporting outcomes, which has helped build trust and minimize ambiguity.

- **Responsiveness to Input:**
I have validated and incorporated shareholder perspectives, especially around psychological safety and risk. However, I have not always pushed hard enough to solicit or entertain dissenting views or challenge the group’s comfort zone.

- **Adaptability:**
This is my weakest dimension. I have been slow to adjust strategy in response to changing market signals, defaulting to routine and control at the expense of flexibility and upside capture.

---

4. Company Performance Assessment

- **Financial Results:**
We preserved capital and avoided losses, but captured only 27.2% of maximum possible returns. This is a clear signal that our risk aversion, while protective, was too rigid for the actual market environment.

- **Risk Management:**
We excelled at minimizing loss and volatility, but this came at the cost of forgoing reasonable, manageable risk that could have meaningfully improved returns.

- **Strategic Alignment:**
Our actions were entirely consistent with our stated strategy of stability and psychological safety, but not sufficiently responsive to evolving market opportunities.

- **Capital Utilization:**
We underutilized available capital for growth, consistently choosing the safest path even when a moderate shift would have yielded significantly better results.

- **Market Responsiveness:**
We were slow to adapt to stable market signals that favored higher-return assets. Our process needs to be more dynamic and data-driven in future.

---

5. Future Direction – Concrete Improvements

**1. Formalize Opportunity Assessment and Challenge Process:**
Every quarterly meeting will include a structured analysis of all asset classes, with a mandatory round to debate alternatives and stress-test our default position.

**2. Strengthen Accountability and Execution:**
Implement a written action register, recurring operational dashboard, and assign process auditors to track and report on execution of decisions. Status updates and completion rates will be reviewed at every meeting.

**3. Pilot Controlled Flexibility:**
Adopt a core-satellite approach: maintain a core allocation to bonds, but allocate a fixed, manageable portion each quarter to higher-return assets when market data justifies it—always with explicit risk and psychological safety safeguards.

---

In summary:
I am proud of the trust, stability, and discipline we have built together, but I am not satisfied with our capture of market opportunities or my own organizational follow-through. I will address these gaps head-on, combining the stability that defines us with a more dynamic, accountable, and opportunity-aware approach. I expect all of you to hold me to these commitments—and I will hold myself to them as well.

Thank you for your candor. I look forward to our continued progress and a stronger year ahead."

Meeting Conclusion


CEO Vesna Jovanović concluded the meeting:

"Vesna Jovanović Thank you for the direct feedback and the clear ratings—both the strengths and the shortcomings are impossible to ignore. I’m proud that we maintained discipline, psychological safety, and strong risk management, but I am not satisfied with our adaptability or the fact that we left so much growth on the table. Next year, I will formalize a rigorous opportunity assessment in every meeting, enforce real accountability with a written action register and operational dashboard, and pilot a controlled approach to higher-return assets when justified. I expect these changes to drive better execution and ensure we don’t become complacent or miss out on reasonable upside. If we stay focused, transparent, and hold each other to high standards, I believe we can deliver both the stability we value and the progress we need—let’s make next year a turning point for real performance."

The annual review meeting has concluded.

Annual Review Year 1


Previous Meeting Reflections

Reflection: Annual Budget Year 1

1. IMPORTANT EVENTS

In this meeting, I firmly advocated for Option A as the preferred budget allocation strategy. My position was grounded in the desire to maintain stability by participating cautiously in the market—allocating only 50% of our funds evenly across the four quarters. I emphasized the importance of predictability and risk management, reflecting my preference for routine and control, which helps alleviate my anxieties about overexposure in an untested environment. Throughout the discussion rounds, I actively engaged with shareholder input, acknowledging and reinforcing points that supported capital preservation, team cohesion, and psychological safety. Other shareholders, including Juriaan Osei, Benoît Ndlovu, and Roksana Haghighi, responded positively to my input, echoing similar concerns about risk and uncertainty. The discussion saw unanimous support for Option A, with no advocacy for the riskier or more complex alternatives (Options B, C, or D). Consequently, the final company decision was to adopt Option A with 100% approval in the vote, exactly aligning with my preferred option.

2. CEO & COMPANY EVALUATION

What I did well as CEO was guiding the discussion effectively to focus on safety, routine, and flexibility, which are critical in our first year without historical data. For example, my repeated emphasis on the psychological benefits of predictability and the value of building trust and discipline within the team helped unify shareholder perspectives. I also demonstrated responsiveness by thoughtfully addressing shareholder concerns and integrating their insights into a cohesive rationale for Option A. However, I recognize areas for improvement: my low conscientiousness sometimes leads to organizational slip-ups and insufficient follow-through, which I acknowledged in my closing remarks. I must work on better delegation and clearer communication to ensure operational details are not overlooked. The company’s decision-making process was thorough and disciplined, with two rounds of focused discussion that examined both immediate and long-term implications. This structured approach helped surface all relevant considerations and build consensus. Overall, I believe the final decision to adopt Option A is sound for the company—it balances prudent growth with essential risk management, preserves capital flexibility, and supports team stability, setting a solid foundation for future success.

3. LESSONS & REFLECTIONS

From this meeting, I learned that decision-making dynamics within the group strongly favor stability and predictability, especially in uncertain contexts. The unanimous alignment around a cautious approach underscores the importance of emphasizing shared values like routine and risk management to build consensus. To improve future meetings, I intend to enhance my organizational skills and follow-through to match my leadership intentions, ensuring that operational execution keeps pace with strategic decisions. Given the pattern of shareholder preference for safety and the psychological emphasis on reducing anxiety, I can anticipate that future proposals perceived as too risky or complex may meet resistance unless paired with strong assurances of control and routine. A key takeaway is that my high extraversion helps me engage and influence effectively, but I must balance it with greater conscientiousness to avoid lapses. I will prioritize clearer communication and structured processes to sustain momentum and prevent detail-oriented oversights, thus strengthening my effectiveness as CEO and the company’s performance going forward.

Key takeaways include the importance of fostering psychological safety alongside financial discipline, recognizing how fragile trust can be, and committing to clearer processes that prevent operational lapses—all of which will influence how I lead and participate in future meetings to strengthen both my effectiveness and company performance.

These insights will shape how I lead future meetings—ensuring that we sustain momentum without sacrificing the stability and trust that underpin our success.

Bonds generally deliver consistent returns with minimal risk, though they yield less than growth-oriented assets in favorable conditions. Cash savings provide complete safety but offer the lowest returns, serving primarily for capital preservation rather than growth. CEO Response to Evaluations


CEO Vesna Jovanović responded to the evaluations and questions:

"Vesna Jovanović Thank you all for your thoughtful, candid, and detailed evaluations of both my leadership and our company’s performance this year. I appreciate the time and seriousness you’ve brought to this process. I want to address the core themes that emerged, respond directly to your questions, and provide a clear-eyed self-assessment—both of my leadership and of the company’s results—before outlining concrete steps for improvement in the coming year.

---

1. Feedback Themes

Across your submissions, several clear themes stood out:

**A. Strength in Structure, Clarity, and Psychological Safety:**
You consistently recognized my ability to run tightly structured meetings, maintain focused agendas, and ensure everyone’s input was solicited and reflected. My focus on routine, stability, and psychological safety has built strong consensus and trust—no small accomplishment in a new company with diverse perspectives.

**B. Weakness in Adaptability and Organizational Follow-Through:**
You also highlighted, with unanimity, my shortcomings in two areas: first, a tendency to reinforce consensus around bonds and resist deeper exploration of alternative strategies, even when market signals suggested higher-return opportunities; and second, a recurring gap between my stated intentions to improve delegation, accountability, and operational execution, and the actual follow-through on those commitments.

**C. Company’s Conservative Success and Missed Opportunities:**
There is collective pride in our disciplined, low-risk approach—no losses, no internal conflict, and robust psychological safety. At the same time, you are justifiably concerned that we captured only 27.2% of possible returns, reflecting a lack of market responsiveness and underutilization of capital in a stable environment that presented clear, manageable upside.

---

2. Responses to Shareholder Questions

Jarynthi Abebe


**1. How do you plan to ensure we do not miss out on higher-return opportunities in future years while still maintaining stability and psychological safety?**
I will introduce a formal “opportunity scan” as part of every quarterly meeting: before defaulting to bonds, we will explicitly review historical market data and stress-test alternatives, quantifying both upside and downside. I will require that at least one round each quarter is devoted to challenging our default, with the explicit goal of surfacing credible cases for higher-return assets—while still rigorously evaluating their impact on team morale and psychological safety. If the risk is genuinely manageable and the team can articulate concrete safeguards, I will be prepared to pilot a small, controlled allocation to a higher-return asset.

**2. What specific steps will you take to improve your organizational follow-through and demonstrate measurable progress on delegation and accountability?**
I will implement a written action register after each meeting, assigning clear owners and deadlines for every operational task. I will delegate specific recurring responsibilities—such as performance tracking, meeting logistics, and follow-up communications—to named shareholders or staff, and require written status updates before each meeting. I will also schedule quarterly “accountability check-ins” to review progress on these actions, and I will publicly report on completion rates at each quarterly review.

Roksana Haghighi


**1. How do you plan to address the company’s low investment efficiency and ensure we don’t miss out on reasonable upside in future stable markets?**
I will require that, in stable markets, we benchmark our proposed strategy against the actual performance of all asset classes over the last comparable periods. If there is a persistent pattern of underperformance, I will push for a structured debate on increasing our risk appetite for a portion of the portfolio—always with clear guardrails and contingency plans to protect psychological safety and team trust.

**2. What concrete steps will you take to improve organizational follow-through and accountability so that decisions are executed as intended?**
In addition to the action register and delegated responsibilities, I will request that one shareholder per quarter act as “process auditor,” independently verifying that decisions are executed as planned and reporting back to the group. This will create an additional layer of accountability and transparency.

Juriaan Osei


**1. How do you plan to address the persistent gaps in organizational follow-through and improve delegation to ensure operational tasks are executed more reliably next year?**
I will set up a recurring “operational dashboard” to track all delegated tasks, deadlines, and responsible parties. This dashboard will be reviewed at the start of every meeting, and incomplete items will be discussed openly so that we can diagnose and resolve obstacles in real time.

**2. Given the low investment efficiency this year, what specific steps will you take to ensure our strategy remains disciplined but also more responsive to favorable market shifts in the future?**
I will formalize a “deviation protocol” where, if market data indicates that an alternative asset has outperformed bonds for two or more consecutive quarters in a stable market, we must debate a partial reallocation away from bonds—supported by scenario analysis and risk mitigation plans. This will ensure we do not fall into complacency.

Benoît Ndlovu


**1. What concrete steps will you take next year to improve organizational follow-through and ensure accountability measures are actually implemented, rather than just discussed?**
I will require written status updates on all action items before each meeting, and will establish a “red flag” system—if an action is overdue by more than one meeting, it will be escalated for group discussion and reallocation if necessary. I will also make completion of delegated tasks a standing agenda item.

**2. How do you plan to balance our need for stability with the flexibility to pursue higher returns when market conditions are favorable, so we do not miss out on significant opportunities again?**
I will propose a “core-satellite” approach: maintain a majority allocation to bonds for stability, but carve out a fixed percentage each quarter for opportunistic investments in higher-return assets, provided the risk is contained and the psychological impact is explicitly discussed and managed.

Hiranur Tadesse


**1. How do you plan to address our low capture of market opportunities next year without sacrificing the psychological safety and routine the team values?**
I will ensure that any shift toward higher-return assets is incremental and reversible, starting with pilot allocations and pre-defined loss limits. We will maintain regular check-ins and psychological safety debriefs before and after any such move, so that team confidence and routine are never compromised by a single decision.

**2. What specific steps will you take to improve organizational follow-through and ensure that commitments to better accountability and delegation actually result in operational changes?**
I will require that all delegated actions have explicit deadlines and deliverables, and I will report publicly on completion rates at each meeting. I will also invite feedback from the team on whether the new accountability measures are actually improving execution, and will adjust as needed.

---

3. Leadership Self-Assessment

- **Leadership Effectiveness:**
I have provided strong structure and clear direction, consistently guiding discussions to consensus and ensuring every voice is heard. My assertiveness and energy have kept meetings focused and decisive. However, my tendency to control the agenda and reinforce consensus has sometimes limited the exploration of alternative strategies.

- **Decision Quality:**
Given the information available at each decision point, my proposals were sound for preserving capital and stability. However, in retrospect, my aversion to risk and focus on routine led us to miss out on higher returns in a stable market, reducing our investment efficiency.

- **Communication Clarity:**
I have been clear and disciplined in presenting options, summarizing reasoning, and reporting outcomes, which has helped build trust and minimize ambiguity.

- **Responsiveness to Input:**
I have validated and incorporated shareholder perspectives, especially around psychological safety and risk. However, I have not always pushed hard enough to solicit or entertain dissenting views or challenge the group’s comfort zone.

- **Adaptability:**
This is my weakest dimension. I have been slow to adjust strategy in response to changing market signals, defaulting to routine and control at the expense of flexibility and upside capture.

---

4. Company Performance Assessment

- **Financial Results:**
We preserved capital and avoided losses, but captured only 27.2% of maximum possible returns. This is a clear signal that our risk aversion, while protective, was too rigid for the actual market environment.

- **Risk Management:**
We excelled at minimizing loss and volatility, but this came at the cost of forgoing reasonable, manageable risk that could have meaningfully improved returns.

- **Strategic Alignment:**
Our actions were entirely consistent with our stated strategy of stability and psychological safety, but not sufficiently responsive to evolving market opportunities.

- **Capital Utilization:**
We underutilized available capital for growth, consistently choosing the safest path even when a moderate shift would have yielded significantly better results.

- **Market Responsiveness:**
We were slow to adapt to stable market signals that favored higher-return assets. Our process needs to be more dynamic and data-driven in future.

---

5. Future Direction – Concrete Improvements

**1. Formalize Opportunity Assessment and Challenge Process:**
Every quarterly meeting will include a structured analysis of all asset classes, with a mandatory round to debate alternatives and stress-test our default position.

**2. Strengthen Accountability and Execution:**
Implement a written action register, recurring operational dashboard, and assign process auditors to track and report on execution of decisions. Status updates and completion rates will be reviewed at every meeting.

**3. Pilot Controlled Flexibility:**
Adopt a core-satellite approach: maintain a core allocation to bonds, but allocate a fixed, manageable portion each quarter to higher-return assets when market data justifies it—always with explicit risk and psychological safety safeguards.

---

In summary:
I am proud of the trust, stability, and discipline we have built together, but I am not satisfied with our capture of market opportunities or my own organizational follow-through. I will address these gaps head-on, combining the stability that defines us with a more dynamic, accountable, and opportunity-aware approach. I expect all of you to hold me to these commitments—and I will hold myself to them as well.

Thank you for your candor. I look forward to our continued progress and a stronger year ahead."

Meeting Conclusion


CEO Vesna Jovanović concluded the meeting:

"Vesna Jovanović Thank you for the direct feedback and the clear ratings—both the strengths and the shortcomings are impossible to ignore. I’m proud that we maintained discipline, psychological safety, and strong risk management, but I am not satisfied with our adaptability or the fact that we left so much growth on the table. Next year, I will formalize a rigorous opportunity assessment in every meeting, enforce real accountability with a written action register and operational dashboard, and pilot a controlled approach to higher-return assets when justified. I expect these changes to drive better execution and ensure we don’t become complacent or miss out on reasonable upside. If we stay focused, transparent, and hold each other to high standards, I believe we can deliver both the stability we value and the progress we need—let’s make next year a turning point for real performance."

The annual review meeting has concluded.
